with links
\chi_{i}:S_{v_{i}}\,\hbox to14.73pt{\vbox to0.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-0.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,2.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 16.71 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{12.07642pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.97 0 L -1.78 2.38 L 0 0 L -1.78 -2.38}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,S_{v_{i+1}}
be a cycle (recall that we allow
v_{i}=v_{i+1}
) of length
n+1
, and suppose that the claim holds for cycles of length
n
. Suppose that
\chi_{0}\notin\mathcal{R}_{E}
. Then

\chi_{n}\circ\ldots\circ\chi_{0}=\chi_{n}\circ\ldots\circ\chi_{1}\circ\chi_{v_{1}}\circ\chi_{v_{1}}^{-1}\circ\chi_{0}=\chi_{n}\circ\ldots\chi_{1}\circ\chi_{v_{1}}\circ B_{\chi_{0}},

and we can proceed with the loop
\chi_{n}\circ\ldots\chi_{1}\circ\chi_{v_{1}}
, starting from an element of
\mathcal{R}_{E}
. Hence, we can assume from the very beginning that
\chi_{0}\in\mathcal{R}_{E}
, i.e.
\chi_{0}=\chi_{v_{1}}
. If
\chi_{1}
is a self-link, then
v_{1}=v_{2}
and we get

\chi_{n}\circ\ldots\circ\chi_{2}\circ\chi_{1}\circ\chi_{v_{1}}=\chi_{n}\circ\ldots\circ\chi_{2}\circ\chi_{v_{1}}\circ\chi_{v_{1}}^{-1}\circ\chi_{1}\circ\chi_{v_{1}}=\chi_{n}\circ\ldots\circ\chi_{2}\circ\chi_{v_{1}}\circ D_{\chi_{1}}.

The cycle
\chi_{n}\circ\ldots\circ\chi_{2}\circ\chi_{v_{1}}
is of length
n
and we are done by the induction hypothesis. If
\chi_{1}
leads back to
S
, then our cycle is equal to
B_{\chi_{1}^{-1}}^{-1}
. Finally, suppose that
v_{1}\neq v_{2}
, then

\chi_{n}\circ\ldots\circ\chi_{2}\circ\chi_{1}\circ\chi_{0}=\chi_{n}\circ\ldots\circ\chi_{2}\circ\chi_{v_{2}}\circ\chi_{v_{2}}^{-1}\circ\chi_{1}\circ\chi_{v_{1}}=\chi_{n}\circ\ldots\circ\chi_{2}\circ\chi_{v_{2}}\circ A_{\chi_{1}}.

The cycle
\chi_{n}\circ\ldots\circ\chi_{2}\circ\chi_{v_{2}}
is of length
n
and we are done by the induction hypothesis.
∎

7.2.
Sextic del Pezzo surfaces of index 3

be a sextic

-del Pezzo surface of index 3. If

is a Sarkisov 3-link (

), then

is a cubic surface with

. So, if

is a rank 3 fibration corresponding to some relation involving
\chi
, then

is a del Pezzo surface of degree 2 or 1, hence
T^{\prime}\,\hbox to14.73pt{\vbox to0.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-0.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 16.71 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{12.07642pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.97 0 L -1.78 2.38 L 0 0 L -1.78 -2.38}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,T
is a blow-up of a point of degree 1 or 2, which is impossible since
\indexx(T)=3
. We conclude that there are no elementary relations in this case.

Since the composition of a Sarkisov link with an automorphism of Mori fibre space is a Sarkisov link, and the inverse of a Sarkisov link is a Sarkisov link, we obtain
trivial relations
given by
\chi_{2}\circ\chi_{1}=\mathrm{id}
and
\chi_{2}^{-1}\circ\alpha\circ\chi_{1}\circ\beta=\mathrm{id}
, where
\chi_{1},\chi_{2}
are Sarkisov links and
\alpha,\beta
are automorphisms of Mori fibre spaces; we also include the relations between isomorphism of Mori fibre spaces in the set of trivial relations.

Proposition 7.7
.

Let
S
be a sextic
G
-del Pezzo of index
3
. Then every relation between the generators
A_{\chi}
,
B_{\chi}
,
C_{\varphi}
,
D_{\varphi}
is a product of conjugates of the following relations.

(1)

The relations between automorphisms given via a product of
C_{\varphi}
or
D_{\varphi}
.

(2)

B_{\chi}=\mathrm{id}
for each
\chi\in\mathcal{R}_{E}
.

(3)

The relations

3a)

A_{\chi_{2}}\circ A_{\chi_{1}}

3b)

B_{\chi_{2}^{-1}}^{-1}\circ B_{\chi_{1}}

3c)

C_{\varphi}\circ C_{\varphi^{-1}}

3d)

D_{\varphi}\circ D_{\varphi^{-1}}

for every two links
\chi_{1}
,
\chi_{2}
satisfying
\chi_{2}\circ\chi_{1}=\mathrm{id}
, and for all self-links and automorphisms
\varphi
.

(4)

For every two links
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
, equivalent via automorphisms
\alpha
and
\beta
, i.e. satisfying
\alpha\circ\chi_{1}\circ\beta=\chi_{2}
, the following relations.

4a)

A_{\chi_{2}^{-1}}\circ D_{\alpha}\circ A_{\chi_{1}}\circ D_{\beta}

4b)

B_{\chi_{2}}^{-1}\circ D_{\alpha}\circ B_{\chi_{1}}\circ C_{\beta}

4c)

C_{\chi_{2}^{-1}}\circ C_{\alpha}\circ C_{\chi_{1}}\circ C_{\beta}

4d)

D_{\chi_{2}^{-1}}\circ D_{\alpha}\circ D_{\chi_{1}}\circ D_{\beta}
.

Proof.

Let
U_{n}^{\prime}\circ\ldots\circ U_{1}^{\prime}=\mathrm{id}
be a relation, where
U_{i}^{\prime}
are of the form
A_{\chi}
,
B_{\chi}
,
C_{\varphi}
,
D_{\varphi}
, or their inverse. By Theorem
2.15
, we can write this as a relation of Sarkisov links and automorphisms and hence as

U_{n}^{\prime}\circ\ldots\circ U_{1}^{\prime}=\psi_{k}^{-1}\circ R_{k}\circ\psi_{k}\circ\ldots\circ\psi_{1}^{-1}\circ R_{1}\circ\psi_{1}=\mathrm{id}

where
R_{i}
are trivial relations and
\psi_{i}
are some birational maps (possibly including identity maps) in
\BirMori(S)
, see
[
LZ20
, Proposition 3.15]
. Each
\psi_{i}^{-1}\circ R_{i}\circ\psi_{i}
is a birational self-map of
S
and therefore it is enough to prove that
\psi^{-1}\circ R\circ\psi
is a product of conjugates of the relations from the statement for
R
a trivial relation and
\psi
a birational map starting from
S
. Note that
\psi
can be uniquely written as
\psi=\chi\circ\psi^{\prime}
, where
\psi^{\prime}\in\Bir(S)
and
\chi\colon S\,\hbox to14.73pt{\vbox to0.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-0.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,2.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 16.71 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{12.07642pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.97 0 L -1.78 2.38 L 0 0 L -1.78 -2.38}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,S_{v}
is a (possibly empty) sequence of Sarkisov links. We then consider possible subcases.

(1)

Case

\chi=\varnothing
. Then
R=U_{i_{1}}\circ\ldots\circ U_{i_{s}}
is a birational self-map of
S
.

\bullet

Suppose that
R
is a composition of automorphisms. Then it is a relation between generators of type
C
, i.e. a relation of type (1).

\bullet

Suppose that
R=\chi_{2}\circ\chi_{1}
. Then
R=B_{\chi_{1}}
is of type (2) if
\chi_{1}\in\mathcal{R}_{E}
,
R=C_{\chi_{2}}\circ C_{\chi_{1}}
is of type (3c) if
\chi_{1}
is a self-link or automorphism, and
R=B_{\chi_{2}^{-1}}^{-1}\circ B_{\chi_{1}}
is of type (3b) if
\chi_{1}
is not a self-link.

\bullet

If
R={\chi_{2}}^{-1}\circ\alpha\circ\chi_{1}\circ\beta
, then
R=C_{\chi_{2}^{-1}}\circ C_{\alpha}\circ C_{\chi_{1}}\circ C_{\beta}
is a relation of type (4c) when
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
are self-links. If they are not, then they have the form
\chi_{1},\chi_{2}\colon S\,\hbox to14.73pt{\vbox to0.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-0.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,2.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 16.71 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{12.07642pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.97 0 L -1.78 2.38 L 0 0 L -1.78 -2.38}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,S_{v}
for some
v\in V
. Thus

{\chi_{2}}^{-1}\circ\alpha\circ\chi_{1}\circ\beta=\underbrace{\chi_{2}^{-1}\circ\chi_{v}}_{B_{\chi_{2}}^{-1}}\circ\underbrace{\chi_{v}^{-1}\circ\alpha\circ\chi_{v}}_{D_{\alpha}}\circ\underbrace{\chi_{v}^{-1}\circ\chi_{1}}_{B_{\chi_{1}}}\circ\underbrace{\beta}_{C_{\beta}}=B_{\chi_{2}}^{-1}\circ D_{\alpha}\circ B_{\chi_{1}}\circ C_{\beta}

which is a relation of type (4b).

(2)

Case

\psi=\chi\circ\psi^{\prime}
, where
\psi^{\prime}\in\Bir(S)
and
\chi\colon S\,\hbox to14.73pt{\vbox to0.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-0.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,2.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 16.71 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{12.07642pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.97 0 L -1.78 2.38 L 0 0 L -1.78 -2.38}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,S_{v}
is some Sarkisov link. Then

\psi^{-1}\circ R\circ\psi={\psi^{\prime}}^{-1}\circ\chi^{-1}\circ R\circ\chi\circ\psi^{\prime}

and hence we need to show that
\chi^{-1}\circ R\circ\chi
is a product of conjugates of relations from the statement. We again consider possible subcases.

\bullet

Suppose that
R
is a composition of automorphisms, say
R=\alpha_{1}\circ\ldots\circ\alpha_{m}
. Then

\chi^{-1}\circ R\circ\chi=B_{\chi}^{-1}\circ D_{\alpha_{1}}\circ\ldots\circ D_{\alpha_{m}}\circ B_{\chi}

it is a relation of type (1).

\bullet

Consider relations
R=\chi_{2}\circ\chi_{1}
, and note that
\chi_{1}
starts at
S_{v}
. Suppose first that
\chi_{1}
goes to
S
. Then

\chi^{-1}\circ\chi_{2}\circ\chi_{1}\circ\chi=B_{\chi}^{-1}\circ B_{\chi_{2}}\circ(B_{\chi_{1}^{-1}})^{-1}\circ B_{\chi}

is a conjugate of type (3b) relation by
B_{\chi}
(since
\chi_{2}\circ\chi_{1}=\mathrm{id}
is equivalent to
\chi_{1}\circ\chi_{2}=\mathrm{id}
).
Now suppose that
\chi_{1}\colon S_{v}\,\hbox to14.73pt{\vbox to0.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-0.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,2.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 16.71 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{12.07642pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.97 0 L -1.78 2.38 L 0 0 L -1.78 -2.38}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,S_{w}
. If
v=w
, then

\chi^{-1}\circ\chi_{2}\circ\chi_{1}\circ\chi=B_{\chi}^{-1}\circ D_{\chi_{2}}\circ D_{\chi_{1}}\circ B_{\chi}

is a conjugate of type (3d) relation by
B_{\chi}
.
If
v\neq w
, then

\chi^{-1}\circ\chi_{2}\circ\chi_{1}\circ\chi=B_{\chi}^{-1}\circ A_{\chi_{2}}\circ A_{\chi_{1}}\circ B_{\chi}.

is a conjugate of type (3a) relation by
B_{\chi}
.

\bullet

Further, we consider relations
R=\chi_{2}^{-1}\circ\alpha\circ\chi_{1}\circ\beta
. Suppose first that
\chi_{1}\colon S_{v}\,\hbox to14.73pt{\vbox to0.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-0.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,2.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 16.71 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{12.07642pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.97 0 L -1.78 2.38 L 0 0 L -1.78 -2.38}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,S
, then
\chi_{2}\colon S_{v}\,\hbox to14.73pt{\vbox to0.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-0.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,2.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 16.71 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{12.07642pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.97 0 L -1.78 2.38 L 0 0 L -1.78 -2.38}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,S
. We have

\chi^{-1}\circ\chi_{2}^{-1}\circ\alpha\circ\chi_{1}\circ\beta\circ\chi=B_{\chi}^{-1}\circ B_{\chi_{2}^{-1}}\circ C_{\alpha}\circ B_{\chi_{1}^{-1}}^{-1}\circ D_{\beta}\circ B_{\chi},

which is a conjugate of type (4b) relation by
B_{\chi}
, because
\chi_{2}=\alpha\circ\chi_{1}\circ\beta
is equivalent to
\chi_{2}^{-1}=\beta^{-1}\circ\chi_{1}^{-1}\circ\beta^{-1}
.

Then suppose that
\chi_{1}\colon S_{v}\,\hbox to14.73pt{\vbox to0.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-0.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,2.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 16.71 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{12.07642pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.97 0 L -1.78 2.38 L 0 0 L -1.78 -2.38}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,S_{v}
,
\chi_{2}\colon S_{v}\,\hbox to14.73pt{\vbox to0.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-0.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,2.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 16.71 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{12.07642pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.97 0 L -1.78 2.38 L 0 0 L -1.78 -2.38}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,S_{v}
. We obtain

\chi^{-1}\circ\chi_{2}^{-1}\circ\alpha\circ\chi_{1}\circ\beta\circ\chi=B_{\chi}^{-1}\circ D_{\chi_{2}^{-1}}\circ D_{\alpha}\circ D_{\chi_{1}}\circ D_{\beta}\circ B_{\chi},

which is a conjugate of type (4d) relation by
B_{\chi}
.

Finally suppose that
\chi_{1}\colon S_{v}\,\hbox to14.73pt{\vbox to0.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-0.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,2.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 16.71 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{12.07642pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.97 0 L -1.78 2.38 L 0 0 L -1.78 -2.38}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,\colon S_{w}
,
v\neq w
, then
\chi_{2}\colon S_{v}\,\hbox to14.73pt{\vbox to0.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-0.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,2.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 16.71 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{12.07642pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.97 0 L -1.78 2.38 L 0 0 L -1.78 -2.38}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,S_{w}
. We have

\chi^{-1}\circ\chi_{2}^{-1}\circ\alpha\circ\chi_{1}\circ\beta\circ\chi=B_{\chi}^{-1}\circ A_{\chi_{2}^{-1}}\circ D_{\alpha}\circ A_{\chi_{1}}\circ D_{\beta}\circ B_{\chi},

which is a conjugate type (4a) relation by
B_{\chi}
.

(3)

Finally, in the general case when
\chi\colon S\,\hbox to14.73pt{\vbox to0.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-0.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,2.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 16.71 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{12.07642pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.97 0 L -1.78 2.38 L 0 0 L -1.78 -2.38}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,S_{v}
is an arbitrary birational map, we can write

\psi^{-1}\circ R\circ\psi=(\chi\circ\psi^{\prime})^{-1}\circ R\circ(\chi\circ\psi^{\prime})=(\chi_{v}^{-1}\circ\chi\circ\psi^{\prime})^{-1}\circ\chi_{v}^{-1}\circ R\circ\chi_{v}\circ(\chi_{v}^{-1}\circ\chi\circ\psi^{\prime}),

and notice that
\chi_{v}^{-1}\circ\chi\circ\psi^{\prime}\in\Bir(S)
, hence it is enough to prove the statement for
\chi_{v}^{-1}\circ R\circ\chi_{v}
. But this case was considered before.

∎

Recall that every edge
e\in E
of
\Graph_{S}
corresponds to the equivalence class of a Sarkisov link
\chi
. Let us denote such edge
e=[\chi]
. Let
\bar{e}=[\chi^{-1}]
be the edge corresponding to the equivalence class of
\chi^{-1}
. If
e=\bar{e}
, i.e.
\chi
is equivalent to
\chi^{-1}
, then we call
\chi
an
almost involution
. Since the vertices of
\Graph_{S}
represent different isomorphism classes of surfaces, amost involutions are self-loops (self-links) at the vertices of
\Graph_{S}
(but a priori not every self-link is an almost involution). Let us label all the edges of
\Graph_{S}
, which are not almost involutions, with signs
+
or
-
, so that for each
e\in E
with
e\neq\bar{e}
the edges
e
and
\bar{e}
have different signs. Given a Sarkisov link
\chi
corresponding to an edge
e
that is not an almost involution, we say that
\chi
is
positive
if
e
has label
+
, and
negative
if
e
has label
-
. Finally, declare the elements of
\mathcal{R}_{E}
positive.

Theorem 7.8
.

Let
S
be a del Pezzo surface of degree 6 with
\rk\Pic(S)=1
and
\indexx(S)=3
. Let
\mathcal{E}_{1}
be the set of positive edges in
\mathcal{G}_{S}
such that corresponding links are not equivalent to their inverses, and let
\mathcal{E}_{2}
be the set of edges such that corresponding links are equivalent to their inverses (note that this is a subset of the set of self-loops at the vertices of
\Graph_{S}
). Then the map

\displaystyle\Psi\colon U_{\chi}\,\hbox to14.73pt{\vbox to5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-2.5pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.52 0 L 16.71 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{-1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{-1.0}{0.25pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.46 L 0 -3.46}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}{{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{12.07642pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.97 0 L -1.78 2.38 L 0 0 L -1.78 -2.38}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,\begin{cases}\ \ 1_{[\chi]}&\text{ where $\chi$ is positive or an almost involution,}\\
-1_{[\chi^{-1}]}&\text{ where $\chi$ is negative,}\\
\ \ \mathrm{id}&\text{ if }\chi\text{ is an automorphism,}\ \chi\in\mathcal{R}_{E}\text{ or }\chi^{-1}\in\mathcal{R}_{E}\end{cases}

defines a surjective non-trivial group homomorphism

\Bir_{\mathbf{k}}(S)\,\hbox to14.73pt{\vbox to0.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-0.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 16.71 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{8.63647pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.97 0 L -1.78 2.38 L 0 0 L -1.78 -2.38}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{12.07642pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.97 0 L -1.78 2.38 L 0 0 L -1.78 -2.38}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,\left(\bigast_{\mathcal{E}_{1}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{E}}\mathbb{Z}\right)\ast\left(\bigast_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}\right).

Proof.

For brevity, let us call a
\mathcal{E}_{1}
-part and a
\mathcal{E}_{2}
-part the respective subgroups (free products) in the target of
\Psi
, defined in the statement. We first comment why the relations of Proposition
7.7
are sent to identity.

The relations of type (1) and (2) are sent to
\mathrm{id}
according to the third line in the definition of
\Psi
. The relations of type (3a) and (3b) involve only two links
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
which are inverse to each other (and are not equivalent to their inverses), hence
[\chi_{1}]
and
[\chi_{2}]
have different signs and the respective relations are sent to
1_{[\chi]}-1_{[\chi]}=0
in the
\mathcal{E}_{1}
-part, where
\chi\in\{\chi_{1},\chi_{2}\}
. If
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
are almost involutions in (3c) or (3d), then this relation is sent onto
1_{[\varphi]}+1_{[\varphi]}\equiv 0
in
\mathcal{E}_{2}
-part, and onto
1_{[\varphi]}-1_{[\varphi]}=0
in
\mathcal{E}_{1}
-part if those are self-links which are not equivalent to their inverses.

The relations of type (4) involve only equivalent links
\chi_{1}\sim\chi_{2}
. Hence, (4a) and (4b) are sent to the identity of the
\mathcal{E}_{1}
-part. If
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
are almost involutions in (4c) or (4d), then this relation is sent onto
1_{[\chi_{1}]}+1_{[\chi_{2}]}\equiv 0
in
\mathcal{E}_{2}
-part, and onto
1_{[\chi_{1}]}-1_{[\chi_{1}]}=0
in
\mathcal{E}_{1}
-part if those are self-links which are not equivalent to their inverses.

Next, we check that the target of
\Psi
is not trivial. If this is not the case, then
\mathcal{E}_{2}=\varnothing
and
\mathcal{E}_{1}=\mathcal{R}_{E}
. Due to Proposition
5.14
, there is a self-link
\chi
on
S
. It cannot be equivalent to its inverse, since
\mathcal{E}_{2}=\varnothing
. Hence, there are two edges
[\chi]
and
[\chi^{-1}]
of different signs in
E
. Choose a positive link. It is not in
\mathcal{R}_{E}
, as this set contains no self-links. Thus
\mathcal{E}_{1}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{E}\neq\varnothing
, a contradiction. The surjectivity of
\Psi
follows from its definition.
∎

Remark 7.9
.

The indexing set
\mathcal{E}_{1}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{E}
in Theorem
7.8
is nonempty when
S
admits at least two distinct birational models besides itself, i.e. the graph
\Graph_{S}
has at least 3 vertices. Indeed, in that case there exist surfaces
S_{u}
and
S_{v}
with
u,v\in V
,
u\neq v
, distinct from
S
, and a positive edge between
u
and
v
, which is not in
\mathcal{R}_{E}
.

7.3.
Sextic del Pezzo surfaces of index 2

Remark 7.11
.

In the course of the proof of Theorem

, we will find the system of relations between the generators

.

Theorem 7.12
.

Let
S
be a del Pezzo surface of degree 6 with
\rk\Pic(S)=1
and
\indexx(S)=2
. Let
\mathcal{E}
be the set of positive edges and almost involutions in the graph
\mathcal{G}_{S}
, and
\mathcal{I}
be the set (possibly empty) of degree 4 points in general position on
S
. Then the map

\displaystyle U_{\chi}\,\hbox to14.73pt{\vbox to5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-2.5pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.52 0 L 16.71 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{-1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{-1.0}{0.25pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.46 L 0 -3.46}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}{{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{12.07642pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.97 0 L -1.78 2.38 L 0 0 L -1.78 -2.38}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,\begin{cases}\ \ 1_{[\chi]}&\text{ where $\chi$ is positive or an almost involution,}\\
\ \ 1_{[\chi^{-1}]}&\text{ where $\chi$ is negative,}\\
\ \ \mathrm{id}&\text{ if }\chi\text{ is an automorphism,}\ \chi\in\mathcal{R}_{E}\text{ or }\chi^{-1}\in\mathcal{R}_{E}\end{cases}

defines a surjective non-trivial group homomorphism

\Bir_{\mathbf{k}}(S)\,\hbox to14.73pt{\vbox to0.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-0.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 16.71 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{8.63647pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.97 0 L -1.78 2.38 L 0 0 L -1.78 -2.38}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{12.07642pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.97 0 L -1.78 2.38 L 0 0 L -1.78 -2.38}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,\left(\bigoplus_{\mathcal{E}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{E}}\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}\right)\ast\left(\bigast_{\mathcal{I}}\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}\right),

Proof.

Let us first notice that Geiser birational involutions do not appear in any non-trivial relations. Now we find the relations between the generators
U
, which come from the relations of Proposition
7.10
. Let
R
be the relation of length 6, given in Proposition
7.10
, which we view as a sequence of links

(7.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{v_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{v_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{v_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{v_{4}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{v_{5}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi_{4}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi_{5}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi_{6}}

We use the notation of the proof of Proposition
7.7
, and again distinguish several cases.

(1)

Suppose that
\chi=\varnothing
, so
Z=S
in the sequence (
7.1
).

\bullet

Suppose that all surfaces in (
7.1
) are isomorphic, i.e.
v=v_{1}=\ldots=v_{5}
. Then

(7.2)

R=C_{\chi_{6}}\circ\ldots\circ C_{\chi_{1}}.

\bullet

Suppose that any two surfaces in (
7.1
) related by a link are different. Then

(7.3)

R=B_{\chi_{6}^{-1}}^{-1}\circ A_{\chi_{5}}\circ B_{\chi_{4}}\circ B_{\chi_{3}^{-1}}^{-1}\circ A_{\chi_{2}}\circ B_{\chi_{1}}

\bullet

Denote
Z=S=S_{v}
. If
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{4}
, or
\chi_{2}
and
\chi_{5}
, or
\chi_{3}
and
\chi_{6}
are self-links, then we get that
R
is equal, respectively, to

(7.4)

\displaystyle B_{{\chi_{6}}^{-1}}^{-1}\circ B_{\chi_{5}}\circ C_{\chi_{4}}\circ B_{\chi_{3}^{-1}}^{-1}\circ B_{\chi_{2}}\circ C_{\chi_{1}}\quad

\displaystyle\text{when}\ v=v_{1}=v_{3}=v_{4},\ v_{1}\neq v_{2},\ v_{2}\neq v_{3},

\displaystyle B_{{\chi_{6}}^{-1}}^{-1}\circ D_{\chi_{5}}\circ B_{\chi_{4}}\circ B_{\chi_{3}^{-1}}^{-1}\circ D_{\chi_{2}}\circ B_{\chi_{1}}\quad

\displaystyle\text{when}\ v_{1}=v_{2}=v_{4}=v_{5},\ v\neq v_{1},\ v_{2}\neq v_{3}.

\displaystyle C_{\chi_{6}}\circ B_{{\chi_{5}}^{-1}}^{-1}\circ B_{\chi_{4}}\circ C_{\chi_{3}}\circ B_{{\chi_{2}}^{-1}}^{-1}\circ B_{\chi_{1}}\quad

\displaystyle\text{when}\ v=v_{2}=v_{3}=v_{5},\ v\neq v_{1},\ v_{1}\neq v_{2}.

(2)

Suppose that
\psi=\chi\circ\psi^{\prime}
, where
\psi^{\prime}\in\Bir(S)
and
\chi\colon S\,\hbox to14.73pt{\vbox to0.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-0.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,2.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 16.71 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{12.07642pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.97 0 L -1.78 2.38 L 0 0 L -1.78 -2.38}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,S_{v}=Z
is a birational map. As in the proof of Proposition
7.7
, it is enough to prove the statement for
\chi
a single Sarkisov link (the general case then can be reduced to this one, as in the part (3) of the aforementioned proof).

\bullet

Suppose that all surfaces in (
7.1
) are isomorphic. Then

(7.5)

\chi^{-1}\circ R\circ\chi=B_{\chi}^{-1}\circ D_{\chi_{6}}\circ\ldots\circ D_{\chi_{1}}\circ B_{\chi}.

\bullet

Suppose that any two surfaces in (
7.1
) related by a link are different. Then

(7.6)

\chi^{-1}\circ R\circ\chi=B_{\chi}^{-1}\circ A_{\chi_{6}}\circ\ldots\circ A_{\chi_{1}}\circ B_{\chi}.

\bullet

Similarly, we get that
\chi^{-1}\circ R\circ\chi
equals

(7.7)

\displaystyle B_{\chi}^{-1}\circ A_{\chi_{6}}\circ A_{\chi_{5}}\circ D_{\chi_{4}}\circ A_{\chi_{3}}\circ A_{\chi_{2}}\circ D_{\chi_{1}}\circ B_{\chi}

\displaystyle\text{when}\ v=v_{1}=v_{3}=v_{4},\ v_{1}\neq v_{2},\ v_{2}\neq v_{3},

\displaystyle B_{\chi}^{-1}\circ A_{\chi_{6}}\circ D_{\chi_{5}}\circ A_{\chi_{4}}\circ A_{\chi_{3}}\circ D_{\chi_{2}}\circ A_{\chi_{1}}\circ B_{\chi}

\displaystyle\text{when}\ v_{1}=v_{2}=v_{4}=v_{5},\ v\neq v_{1},\ v_{2}\neq v_{3}.

\displaystyle B_{\chi}^{-1}\circ D_{\chi_{6}}\circ A_{\chi_{5}}\circ A_{\chi_{4}}\circ D_{\chi_{3}}\circ A_{\chi_{2}}\circ A_{\chi_{1}}\circ B_{\chi}

\displaystyle\text{when}\ v=v_{2}=v_{3}=v_{5},\ v\neq v_{1},\ v_{1}\neq v_{2}.

Notice that if two links in the sequence
Z\,\hbox to14.73pt{\vbox to0.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-0.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,2.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 16.71 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{12.07642pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.97 0 L -1.78 2.38 L 0 0 L -1.78 -2.38}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,\ldots\,\hbox to14.73pt{\vbox to0.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-0.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,2.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 16.71 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{12.07642pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.97 0 L -1.78 2.38 L 0 0 L -1.78 -2.38}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,S_{v_{3}}
are self-links, then all links in (
7.1
) are. This gives all relations.

To check that that the homomorphism
\Psi
is well defined, we need to prove that all the relations are sent to the identity. For the relations like in Proposition
7.7
this follows similarly as in Theorem
7.8
. For the relations found above, we observe that every
U
appears exactly twice and thus the relation is sent to the identity. The non-triviality follows from the existence of self-links given in Proposition
5.15
.
∎
